## Performance Workload Epochs

The original gate froze `semantic_overload.cpp` but selected live `dev/src`
headers. That made its source stable while allowing the project-header workload
to drift. Those results remain valid within their original epoch, including the
Config candidate/parent comparison, but must not be compared with the corrected
frozen-header epoch.

|---|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|

| frozen project headers | `9764b3835` | frozen source plus the 51-file closure under `benchmarks/self_compile/stable/include` | `/tmp/cppgm-boost-frontier-v2-frozen-header-epoch-9764b3835.json` | 263,559,800,479 | 1,292,648,448 B | 1,009,885,184 B | current immutable epoch baseline; candidate-only record, no parent rerun |

Add one row per coherent compiler fix. Keep detailed logs in `/tmp`; keep the
stable command, diagnostic, reducer, validation, and measured deltas here.

| Status | Suite/target | Root cause and typed fix | Owner regression | Pre-fix evidence | Validation | Perf vs fixed baseline | Commit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| fixed | Function `stateless_test` class-specific placement new | Class-scope allocation and deallocation functions are static members by language rule even when their declarations omit `static`. The class collector treated such functions as ordinary methods, prepended `this`, and rejected placement new's two explicit arguments against the resulting three-parameter binding. A shared typed-name classifier now supplies the language-mandated static fact to ordinary, reference, dependent, inline/out-of-line definition, function-template, and semantic-output paths. No source text, cache, ABI spelling, or Boost-specific rule is added. | `pa16/tests/general/300-class-specific-placement-new.t`, the existing header-free class-placement-new reducer, now omits the redundant `static` while retaining its checked-in LowIR unchanged | The initial Function graph reports both `stateless_integer_add::operator new` candidates as methods; the placement overload is rejected because its fabricated implicit object cannot bind. The exact strict direct compile of `stateless_test.cpp` reproduces the diagnostic before the fix and succeeds afterward. | PA16 focused and direct reports pass `172/172`; configured strict suites pass; PA16 placement/hygiene has zero findings; all 23 text-reparse categories and all 22 audit/perf tests pass. Normal, all ten individual cache-disabled, and all-disabled LowIR are byte-identical with SHA-256 `af0e40368c07efefe75e5d310e9f5424a17ca1ccc3561e07c5303392910e61b7`. Clang 22 and GCC 15 accept and run the reducer warning-clean, both changed production units compile warning-clean under GCC 15, the PA9-excluded broad report passes `3997/3997`, and the exact final Function graph passes. | -1.26% instructions, +1.71% RSS, +2.23% footprint against immutable frozen compiler-and-51-header epoch `9764b3835`; report `/tmp/cppgm-boost-frontier-v2-function-candidate.json`; no parent or live-header command was run | `(this commit)` |
| fixed | Flyweight `test_multictor` initializer-list assignment | Overload ranking treated two `std::initializer_list<T>` parameters as tied after establishing that both were list-initialization sequences. The candidate record now retains the typed standard-conversion rank of each braced element and compares the two element-rank vectors by dominance, so an exact `int` or `unsigned int` element conversion wins over the competing conversion. Pack-expanded braced elements use the existing structured expansion service. No text, cache, symbol, ABI, or Boost-specific path is added. | `pa25/tests/general/200-initializer-list-element-conversion-ranking.t`, a header-free unevaluated assignment reducer at PA25's initializer-list overload owner | The initial graph reports an ambiguous assignment in `test_multictor`; the reduced `decltype(get_box() = {1})` and unsigned counterpart select no unique result before the fix, while Clang 22 and GCC 15 select the exact element type. | PA25 passes `77/77`; the combined PA20/PA25 report passes `161/161`; configured strict suites, PA25 placement/hygiene, all 23 zero-reparse categories, all 22 audit/perf tests, twelve byte-identical cache modes, Clang/GCC warning controls, GCC 15 production builds, the PA9-excluded `3997/3997` report, focused target, and exact final Flyweight graph pass. | measured with the complete Flyweight closure: -1.46% instructions, +1.39% RSS, +2.18% footprint against immutable frozen compiler-and-51-header epoch `9764b3835`; report `/tmp/cppgm-boost-frontier-v2-flyweight-candidate.json`; no parent or live-header command was run | `(this commit)` |
| fixed | Flyweight `test_concurrent_factory` typed-initializer recursion | Speculative constant evaluation of nested rvalue construction could route from class typed-initializer evaluation back into the exact same initializer AST and normalized target type. With no semantic progress or cycle check, the compiler recursed for more than 17,000 frames and crashed. A thread-local RAII guard now rejects only exact active `(CppAstNode, Type)` re-entry as a soft constant-evaluation failure, leaving distinct subobjects and target types unaffected. At-risk candidates now snapshot typed owner, canonical member name, function type, ref qualifier, source-template declaration identity, and template-instantiation key; every later dereference first checks the live-binding set and reacquires only an exactly matching binding through the existing typed lookup path. | fixed | Compat forwarded function designators in constexpr invocation | The constant-value domain had no function-designator value, so passing a function name either failed evaluation or decayed it early and lost the reference-binding category. Function designators now carry typed symbol identity as their own constant kind, bind to function references, and decay only through conversion. Constant calls accept analyzed indirect callees and resolve the stored function identity; direct candidates whose function types remain template-dependent are ignored. Pack expansion preserves parameter id-expressions and their semantic type instead of literalizing them. | fixed | Compat forwarded member-pointer constant invocation | Constant evaluation had no member-pointer value kind, so `&X::member` and `&X::function` could not survive wrapper storage and forwarding into `.*`/`->*` or a member-function call. Adding the typed member-pointer value exposed two existing resolution-order defects: a cached function-template specialization could be reused across incompatible base and derived pointer argument types, and a shared internal symbol could identify the wrong concrete specialization before the object symbol was considered. Member-pointer values now carry the exact semantic type and member identity; access and invocation use typed owner conversion and the implicit object; address values preserve their existing aggregate snapshot; direct-call reuse rejects pointer/member-pointer parameter mismatches; resolved calls use the indexed internal symbol first and fall back to object-symbol lookup only when validation fails. The unary semantic retry is restricted to `&`, the only new required case. No source or semantic text is parsed, and no cache, ABI spelling recovery, witness rule, or Boost-specific path is added. | `pa26/tests/general/300-constexpr-forwarded-member-pointer-derived-object.t` covers data and const member-function pointers stored in wrappers, forwarded first through a base pointer and then through a derived pointer to exercise specialization ordering | Before the fix the four exact Compat member-pointer constexpr targets fail constant evaluation. | fixed | Compat contained-object virtual-base forwarding | Hidden virtual-base argument lowering treated a path rooted in reference storage as a hosted polymorphic reference before considering that the path had crossed into a by-value class member. | fixed | Concept Check function-address non-type template arguments | Binding a concrete function as a non-type template argument recorded the selected function identity but did not require semantic validation of its definition. Boost's concept wrapper takes the address of `requirement_<Model>::failed`, whose body calls `Model`'s destructor; skipping both bodies made invalid models compile successfully. Selected function-valued arguments now enter a deferred semantic-only validation queue after template collection. The queue validates explicit special-member bodies and recursively follows called functions without requesting output closure, materialization, class output, or emitted-function ordering. No source text, rendered symbol, cache recovery, or Boost-specific path is used. | `pa23/tests/general/300-function-address-nttp-instantiates-member-body-bad.t`, at PA23's integrated non-type-template instantiation owner, mirrors the standard wrapper/destructor shape and requires a compile failure when the model lacks its requested member | Before the fix seven Concept Check negative targets were unexpected successes. Immediate validation was insufficient because a speculative reference pass could consume the diagnostic and a later cached class selection could bypass revalidation; the deferred post-collection queue makes validation deterministic. Clang 22 and GCC 15 reject the owner reducer. | Warning-clean production build and isolated Clang/GCC translation-unit checks; PA21/PA23/PA26 report `710/710`; all configured strict suites pass; PA23 placement has zero findings; all 23 text-reparse categories and all 23 audit tests pass. ## Decision Log

- `2026-07-20`: Closed GIL after correcting an upstream Boost 1.91 Jamfile
  collision and advanced the ordered cursor to suite 50, `libs/graph/test`.
  `core/io/Jamfile` declared compile-only and run actions for the same
  `path_spec.cpp` implicit target, producing `No best alternative`; the exact
  duplication is also present in the Boost 1.91 tag and current GIL `develop`.
  Removing the redundant compile-only action retains the stronger run action.
  The corrected C++11 graph completes every configuration check, finds only its
  bookkeeping target because C++14 constexpr is unavailable, and exits
  successfully. No compiler change, regression update, broad rerun, or
  performance measurement was required.

- `2026-07-20`: Closed Geometry without a compiler change and advanced the
  ordered cursor to suite 49, `libs/gil/test`. The exact four-job graph
  completes its configuration checks, finds the single bookkeeping target,
  and exits successfully. Both C++14 constexpr configurations are unavailable
  in the C++11 lane, so no regression, broad repository rerun, or performance
  measurement was required.

- `2026-07-20`: Closed Fusion and advanced the ordered cursor to suite 48,
